CUSTO HOUS8E, NEWCHWANG, 9TH JANUARY 1902o SIR, .-TJhe, Imperial Russian Provisional Civil Administration havin opened, on the 12th August igoo,l ustom House to collect, under Foreign supervision, Dues and Likin on Junk Cargoes, I now transmit the statistia ot that trade from iat October to 31at December igoi. On all cargoes arriving or leaving by sea-going junks Dues and Duties were collected at Treaty Trit rat and Likin at the rate of A per cent. ad alorem on Imports and A per cent, ad valqrem on Exports, with the exception of the Duties on certain specified staples, which were reduced on ast May igoi to the rate formerly assessed by the Native Cnstoms. Boats trading up river paid Tonnage Dues, but no Duties were levied on their cargoes. II. (Beeived 13th January x9o2.) CUSTOM HOUSE, KIAOCHOW, 9TH JANUARY 1902. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in you Circulars NOS. 21 of 1869, 14 of 1872, and &amp;amp; Of 1$75, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the fourth quarter of the year 190. b. The Custom House (M X M) was opened on the ist July 1899. The collection of Tariff Import and Export Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.2T 30 + 8o per picul), and the collection of Native Duties and ihin on Junk Cargoes at half Tariff rates, commenced on that day. The stations at present provisionally controlled the Kiaochow Customs are known as Taputon ( ); H iysi (H h); Lingshanwei ( ( g,including Tachiagkou(A Q); Nuku(2k ); Tsangkou (Q I); and ShatsTkon ( ) including Tengyao ($ ). c. The word &quot; Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China, the statistics of goods landed and consumed in German territory not being included. 1901.] NOTvIC4ATIONS. 229 From Cape Terminal the rock bears N. 57* E., true, distant 2 miles, and is in latitude 39*. 10&#39; 28&quot; north and longitude 1220 12&#39; 8&quot; east; it lies on the continuation of a line joining. Cape Terminal and Triple Island. The rock is about 12 feet in diameter, and so steep-to that at a distance of from 30, to 40 feet from it there is a depth of over 5 fathoms, which rapidly increases to 8 and 9 fathoms. The position of the rock is temporarily indicated by a red spar-buoy moored in I8 feet of water off its northern edge. (Signed) BOISMAN, Captain, ist Rank, acting as Marine Comminioner. I A APPENDIX 1. KOWLOON AND LAPPA: QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE. OOTOBER-DECEMBER 1901. 3
(Received 16th Janitary 19go2.) CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, gT JAwUARY 190. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained*in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transm to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouria stations in Native vessels for the fourth quarter of the year 190. b. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are Taishan ( i), i and Shamchun ( )), on the north and wes; Samun ( f ), on the east; and Shaiichung a Shatowkok ( V il), on the Mirs Bay littoral. The Custom House is called IL it I. e. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics neans to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China. II.
